# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

68125 [main] INFO  du.ustb.sei.mde.mwe2.EcoreGenerator  - LLM Response:
{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@24c685e7 (name: calculateAverageBudget) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary:
  Computes the average budget amount of all projects that belong to a
  single department. The average is the arithmetic mean of every
  project's budget in this department.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  if projects is empty:
      return 0.0
  total = 0
  for each project p in this.projects:
      total += p.budget
  average = total / projects.size()
  return average
Related Features:
  - projects (the department's list of projects)
Related Classes:
  Department, Project
Input:
  - None

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
Summary:
  Computes the average budget amount of all projects that belong to a
  single department. The average is the arithmetic mean of every
  project's budget in this department.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  if projects is empty:
      return 0.0
  total = 0
  for each project p in this.projects:
      total += p.budget
  average = total / projects.size()
  return average
Related Features:
  - projects (the department's list of projects)
Related Classes:
  Department, Project
Input:
  - None
Output:
  - double
      The arithmetic mean of the budgets of all projects stored in
      the department, in the same currency / unit as Project.budget.
      Returns 0.0 when the department has no projects.
Pre-condition:
  - The Department instance has been initialized.
  - projects may be empty.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the department is unchanged.
  - The returned double equals (sum of Project.budget) / (number of
    projects), or 0.0 when there are no projects.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
Summary:
  Retrieves the funding group type (PRIVATE / GOVERNMENT / MIXED) of
  every community project that belongs to the given department. The
  result preserves the iteration order of the projects list.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  result = new List<FundingGroupType>()
  for each project p in this.projects:
      if p instanceof CommunityProject:
          result.add(((CommunityProject)p).fundingGroup.type)
  return result
Related Features:
  - projects (the department's list of projects)
Related Classes:
  Department, Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
Input:
  - None
Output:
  - List<FundingGroupType>
      A list of funding group types, one entry per community project
      found in the department. The list is empty when the department
      contains no community project. The order matches the order of
      projects as they appear in Department.projects.
Pre-condition:
  - The Department instance has been initialized.
  - Every CommunityProject in projects has a non-null fundingGroup
    whose type is set; otherwise the corresponding entry in the result
    list will be null.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the department is unchanged.
  - The size of the returned list equals the number of CommunityProject
    instances inside Department.projects.

# Operation Specifications
Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary:
  Counts the distinct number of employees who work on at least one
  production project anywhere in the company. An employee who is
  assigned to multiple production projects is counted only once.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  distinctEmployees = new Set<Employee>()
  for each department d in this.departments:
      for each project p in d.projects:
          if p instanceof ProductionProject:
              for each employee e in p.workingEmployees:
                  distinctEmployees.add(e)
  return distinctEmployees.size()
Related Features:
  - departments (the company-wide list of departments)
Related Classes:
  Company, Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input:
  - None
Output:
  - int
      A non-negative integer representing the number of distinct
      employees (by identity) who are listed in the workingEmployees
      of at least one ProductionProject. Returns 0 when no production
      project exists or no employee is assigned to any of them.
Pre-condition:
  - The Company instance has been initialized.
  - Departments, projects and workingEmployees lists may be empty.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the company is unchanged.
  - The returned integer equals the cardinality of the set of employees
    that participate in at least one ProductionProject.

Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary:
  Computes the sum of the budget amounts of every project belonging to
  every department of the company. The result represents the total budget
  of all projects in the company.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  total = 0
  for each department d in this.departments:
      for each project p in d.projects:
          total += p.budget
  return total
Related Features:
  - departments (the company-wide list of departments)
Related Classes:
  Company, Department, Project
Input:
  - None
Output:
  - double
      The aggregated sum of every project's budget across every
      department, expressed in the same currency / unit as Project.budget.
      If the company has no projects, the returned value is 0.0.
Pre-condition:
  - The Company instance has been initialized.
  - departments may be empty (in which case the returned value is 0.0)
    and projects lists may be empty for any individual department.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the company is unchanged.
  - The returned double equals the arithmetic sum of the budget fields
    of all Project objects reachable from the departments list.

Company::addDepartment
Summary:
  Adds a new department to the company, while enforcing the rule that the
  company must always have between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). The new
  department must have a non-null, non-empty, and unique name (uniqueness is
  compared with the existing department IDs/names already stored in the
  company). If any of these constraints is violated, an exception is thrown
  and the company state remains unchanged.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  1. Validate input: department != null and department.name/ID not null
     and not empty.
  2. If validation fails, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException
     ("Maximum number of departments reached").
  4. For each existing d in departments:
       if d.getID().equals(department.getID()) (or d.getName()):
           throw IllegalArgumentException("Duplicate department name").
  5. Add department to departments list.
  6. Set the back-reference department.company = this.
Related Features:
  - departments (the collection that stores Department objects in Company)
Related Classes:
  Company, Department
Input:
  - department : Department
      The department instance to be added. Must be non-null and must carry
      a valid, non-empty, unique identifier (ID/name).
Output:
  - void
      No value is returned; the department is stored in the company's
      departments list as a side-effect.
Pre-condition:
  - The caller has supplied a non-null Department with a non-null,
    non-empty ID/name.
  - The current number of departments in the company is strictly less
    than 8.
  - The supplied department's ID/name does not duplicate any existing
    department's ID/name in the company.
Post-condition:
  - The supplied department is appended to Company.departments.
  - The department's back-reference (department.company) is set to the
    calling Company instance.
  - The total number of departments is between 2 and 8 (inclusive)
    after a successful addition.
  - If any rule is violated, the company state is unchanged and the
    appropriate exception is propagated.

Company::removeDepartment
Summary:
  Removes an existing department from the company, identified by its
  name (or ID). The operation enforces that the company must always keep
  between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive); therefore a removal is only
  allowed when more than 2 departments currently exist. The method also
  validates the supplied name and the existence of the target department.
Algorithm / Pseudo-code:
  1. Validate input: name != null and !name.isEmpty(); if not,
     throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. If departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException
     ("Minimum number of departments reached").
  3. Find d in departments such that d.getID()/getName() equals name.
     If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException
     ("Department does not exist").
  4. Remove d from departments.
  5. Return the name of the removed department (or its identifier).
Related Features:
  - departments (the collection that stores Department objects in Company)
Related Classes:
  Company, Department
Input:
  - name : String
      The identifier (ID/name) of the department to remove. Must be
      non-null and non-empty, and must match exactly one existing
      department.
Output:
  - String
      The name (ID) of the department that was successfully removed,
      allowing the caller to confirm which department has been detached.
      The value is undefined when an exception is thrown.
Pre-condition:
  - The supplied name is non-null and non-empty.
  - The company currently holds strictly more than 2 departments.
  - A department whose ID/name equals the supplied name actually
    exists in departments.
Post-condition:
  - On success: the matching department is no longer present in
    Company.departments and the returned String contains its name.
  - The remaining number of departments is still within [2, 8].
  - On failure: the company state is unchanged and the corresponding
    IllegalArgumentException or IllegalStateException is thrown.

21:08:15.237 [main] DEBUG org.springframework.web.client.DefaultRestClient -- Writing [ChatCompletionRequest[messages=[ChatCompletionMessage[rawContent=# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double

    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}

    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}

    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
Summary: Calculates the average budget amount of all projects within this department.
Algorithm:
  1. If projects is null or projects.isEmpty(), return 0.0.
  2. Initialize sum = 0.0.
  3. For each project p in this.projects:
       a. sum += p.budget.
  4. Return sum / projects.size().
Related Features: projects : List<Project>
Related Classes: Project
Input: None
Output: double - the average budget of all projects in this department. Value range: >= 0.0 (assuming non-negative budgets).
Pre-condition:
  - The department has been initialized with a non-null projects list.
  - Each project in the list has a valid (non-negative) budget value.
Post-condition:
  - The returned value equals the arithmetic mean of all project budgets in this department, or 0.0 if the department has no projects.
  - No state of the department is modified.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
Summary: Retrieves the funding group types of all community projects within this department.
Algorithm:
  1. Create an empty List<FundingGroupType> result.
  2. For each project p in this.projects:
       a. If p instanceof CommunityProject:
            i. If ((CommunityProject) p).fundingGroup is not null, add ((CommunityProject) p).fundingGroup.type to result.
  3. Return result.
Related Features: projects : List<Project>
Related Classes: Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType
Input: None
Output: List<FundingGroupType> - a list of funding group types (PRIVATE, GOVERNMENT, MIXED) for every community project in this department. May be empty if the department has no community projects or if their funding groups are null. Order follows the iteration order of the projects list.
Pre-condition:
  - The department has been initialized with a non-null projects list.
  - Each community project in the list may have an associated fundingGroup whose type is a valid FundingGroupType enum value.
Post-condition:
  - The returned list contains the funding group type for each community project in the department that has a non-null fundingGroup.
  - No state of the department is modified.

# Operation Specifications
Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production project anywhere in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.
Algorithm:
  1. Create an empty Set<Employee> uniqueEmployees (e.g., HashSet).
  2. For each department d in this.departments:
       a. For each project p in d.projects:
            i. If p instanceof ProductionProject:
                 - For each employee e in p.workingEmployees:
                     * Add e to uniqueEmployees.
  3. Return uniqueEmployees.size().
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input: None
Output: int - the number of distinct employees that work on at least one production project. Value range: >= 0.
Pre-condition:
  - The company has been initialized with a non-null departments list.
  - Each department has a non-null projects list.
  - Each project has a non-null workingEmployees list.
Post-condition:
  - The returned value equals the number of unique employees associated with at least one production project.
  - No state of the company is modified.

Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary: Calculates the total budget of all projects across all departments of the company by summing the budget of every project contained in every department.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each department d in this.departments:
       a. For each project p in d.projects:
            i. total += p.budget.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input: None
Output: double - the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments. Value range: >= 0.0 (assuming non-negative budgets).
Pre-condition:
  - The company has been initialized with a non-null departments list (containing at least 2 departments per the domain rule).
  - Each department in the list contains a non-null projects list.
  - Each project has a valid (non-negative) budget value.
Post-condition:
  - The returned value equals the sum of all project budgets in all departments.
  - No state of the company is modified.

Company::addDepartment
Summary: Adds a new department to the company, enforcing the constraint that the company must always have between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). The operation validates the input and the current state of the company before performing the addition.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate the input department:
     a. If department is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
     b. If department.name is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
     c. Iterate over the current departments list; if any existing department has the same name (case-insensitive), throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Validate the current state:
     a. If departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
  3. Set the back-reference: department.company = this.
  4. Append the department to the departments list.
  5. Return.
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department
Input:
  - department : Department - the department instance to add. Must be non-null and must have a non-null, non-empty, unique name within the company.
Output: void
Pre-condition:
  - The company currently has fewer than 8 departments (i.e., departments.size() < 8).
  - The provided department is non-null, has a non-null/non-empty name, and no existing department in the company shares the same name.
Post-condition:
  - The provided department is appended to the departments list.
  - The back-reference department.company is set to this company.
  - The total number of departments is increased by 1 and remains within [2, 8].

Company::removeDepartment
Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by its name, enforcing the constraint that the company must always have between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). The operation validates the input and the current state of the company before performing the removal.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate the input:
     a. If name is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Validate the current state:
     a. If departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
  3. Search for the department to remove:
     a. Iterate over departments; locate the department whose name equals name (case-insensitive).
     b. If no such department is found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Remove the located department from the departments list.
  5. Clear the back-reference: removedDepartment.company = null.
  6. Return the name of the removed department.
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department
Input:
  - name : String - the name of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty; a department with this name must exist in the company.
Output: String - the name of the department that was successfully removed.
Pre-condition:
  - The company currently has more than 2 departments (i.e., departments.size() > 2).
  - The provided name is non-null and non-empty.
  - A department with the given name exists in the company.
Post-condition:
  - The matching department is removed from the departments list.
  - The removed department's company back-reference is set to null.
  - The total number of departments is decreased by 1 and remains within [2, 8].
  - The name of the removed department is returned.

# Operation Specifications
Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production project anywhere in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.
Algorithm:
  1. Create an empty Set<Employee> uniqueEmployees (e.g., HashSet).
  2. For each department d in this.departments:
       a. For each project p in d.projects:
            i. If p instanceof ProductionProject:
                 - For each employee e in p.workingEmployees:
                     * Add e to uniqueEmployees.
  3. Return uniqueEmployees.size().
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
Input: None
Output: int - the number of distinct employees that work on at least one production project. Value range: >= 0.
Pre-condition:
  - The company has been initialized with a non-null departments list.
  - Each department has a non-null projects list.
  - Each project has a non-null workingEmployees list.
Post-condition:
  - The returned value equals the number of unique employees associated with at least one production project.
  - No state of the company is modified.

Company::calculateTotalBudget
Summary: Calculates the total budget of all projects across all departments of the company by summing the budget of every project contained in every department.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.0.
  2. For each department d in this.departments:
       a. For each project p in d.projects:
            i. total += p.budget.
  3. Return total.
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department, Project
Input: None
Output: double - the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments. Value range: >= 0.0 (assuming non-negative budgets).
Pre-condition:
  - The company has been initialized with a non-null departments list (containing at least 2 departments per the domain rule).
  - Each department in the list contains a non-null projects list.
  - Each project has a valid (non-negative) budget value.
Post-condition:
  - The returned value equals the sum of all project budgets in all departments.
  - No state of the company is modified.

Company::addDepartment
Summary: Adds a new department to the company, enforcing the constraint that the company must always have between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). The operation validates the input and the current state of the company before performing the addition.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate the input department:
     a. If department is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
     b. If department.name is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
     c. Iterate over the current departments list; if any existing department has the same name (case-insensitive), throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Validate the current state:
     a. If departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
  3. Set the back-reference: department.company = this.
  4. Append the department to the departments list.
  5. Return.
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department
Input:
  - department : Department - the department instance to add. Must be non-null and must have a non-null, non-empty, unique name within the company.
Output: void
Pre-condition:
  - The company currently has fewer than 8 departments (i.e., departments.size() < 8).
  - The provided department is non-null, has a non-null/non-empty name, and no existing department in the company shares the same name.
Post-condition:
  - The provided department is appended to the departments list.
  - The back-reference department.company is set to this company.
  - The total number of departments is increased by 1 and remains within [2, 8].

Company::removeDepartment
Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by its name, enforcing the constraint that the company must always have between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). The operation validates the input and the current state of the company before performing the removal.
Algorithm:
  1. Validate the input:
     a. If name is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Validate the current state:
     a. If departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
  3. Search for the department to remove:
     a. Iterate over departments; locate the department whose name equals name (case-insensitive).
     b. If no such department is found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Remove the located department from the departments list.
  5. Clear the back-reference: removedDepartment.company = null.
  6. Return the name of the removed department.
Related Features: departments : List<Department>
Related Classes: Department
Input:
  - name : String - the name of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty; a department with this name must exist in the company.
Output: String - the name of the department that was successfully removed.
Pre-condition:
  - The company currently has more than 2 departments (i.e., departments.size() > 2).
  - The provided name is non-null and non-empty.
  - A department with the given name exists in the company.
Post-condition:
  - The matching department is removed from the departments list.
  - The removed department's company back-reference is set to null.
  - The total number of departments is decreased by 1 and remains within [2, 8].
  - The name of the removed department is returned.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.

  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

  1.4 Generate the class summarization.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.

   * Summary: Calculates the total budget of all projects across all departments of the company by summing the budget of every project contained in every department.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Initialize total = 0.0.
   *   2. For each department d in this.departments:
   *        a. For each project p in d.projects:
   *             i. total += p.budget.
   *   3. Return total.
   * Related Features: departments : List<Department>
   * Related Classes: Department, Project
   * Input: None
   * Output: double - the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments. Value range: >= 0.0 (assuming non-negative budgets).
   * Pre-condition:
   *   - The company has been initialized with a non-null departments list (containing at least 2 departments per the domain rule).
   *   - Each department in the list contains a non-null projects list.
   *   - Each project has a valid (non-negative) budget value.
   * Post-condition:
   *   - The returned value equals the sum of all project budgets in all departments.
   *   - No state of the company is modified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalBudget()
  {
    double total = 0.0;
    for (Department d : this.getDepartments())
    {
      for (Project p : d.getProjects())
      {
        total += p.getBudget();
      }
    }
    return total;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production project anywhere in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.
   * Algorithm:

   *   3. Return uniqueEmployees.size().
   * Related Features: departments : List<Department>

   * Input: None

   *   - Each project has a non-null workingEmployees list.

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public int countEmployeesInProductionProjects()
  {
    Set<Employee> uniqueEmployees = new HashSet<Employee>();
    for (Department d : this.getDepartments())
    {
      for (Project p : d.getProjects())
      {
        if (p instanceof ProductionProject)
        {
          for (Employee e : p.getWorkingEmployees())
          {
            uniqueEmployees.add(e);
          }
        }
      }
    }
    return uniqueEmployees.size();
  }

   *      a. If departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
   *   3. Set the back-reference: department.company = this.
   *   4. Append the department to the departments list.
   *   5. Return.
   * Related Features: departments : List<Department>

   * Input:

   * Post-condition:
   *   - The provided department is appended to the departments list.
   *   - The back-reference department.company is set to this company.
   *   - The total number of departments is increased by 1 and remains within [2, 8].
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
  {
    // 1. Validate the input department
    if (department == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department must not be null.");
    }
    if (department.getID() == null || department.getID().isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department ID must not be null or empty.");
    }
    for (Department existing : this.getDepartments())
    {
      if (existing.getID() != null && existing.getID().equals(department.getID()))
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("A department with the same ID already exists.");
      }
    }
    // 2. Validate the current state
    if (this.getDepartments().size() >= 8)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("The company already has the maximum number of departments (8).");
    }
    // 3. Set the back-reference
    department.setCompany(this);
    // 4. Append the department to the departments list
    this.getDepartments().add(department);
  }

   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by its ID, enforcing the constraint that the company must always have between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). The operation validates the input and the current state of the company before performing the removal.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. Validate the input:
   *      a. If id is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *   2. Validate the current state:
   *      a. If departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
   *   3. Search for the department to remove:
   *      a. Iterate over departments; locate the department whose ID equals id.
   *      b. If no such department is found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *   4. Remove the located department from the departments list.
   *   5. Clear the back-reference: removedDepartment.company = null.
   *   6. Return the ID of the removed department.
   * Related Features: departments : List<Department>
   * Related Classes: Department
   * Input:
   *   - id : String - the ID of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty; a department with this ID must exist in the company.
   * Output: String - the ID of the department that was successfully removed.
   * Pre-condition:
   *   - The company currently has more than 2 departments (i.e., departments.size() > 2).
   *   - The provided id is non-null and non-empty.
   *   - A department with the given id exists in the company.
   * Post-condition:
   *   - The matching department is removed from the departments list.
   *   - The removed department's company back-reference is set to null.
   *   - The total number of departments is decreased by 1 and remains within [2, 8].
   *   - The ID of the removed department is returned.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String removeDepartment(final String name)
  {
    // 1. Validate the input
    if (name == null || name.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department ID must not be null or empty.");
    }
    // 2. Validate the current state
    if (this.getDepartments().size() <= 2)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("The company already has the minimum number of departments (2).");
    }
    // 3. Search for the department to remove
    Department toRemove = null;
    for (Department d : this.getDepartments())
    {
      if (name.equals(d.getID()))
      {
        toRemove = d;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (toRemove == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("No department with the given ID exists in the company.");
    }
    // 4. Remove the located department from the departments list
    this.getDepartments().remove(toRemove);
    // 5. Clear the back-reference
    toRemove.setCompany(null);
    // 6. Return the ID of the removed department
    return toRemove.getID();
  }

  public double calculateAverageBudget()
